        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.

        Plan:

# Implementation Plan: Pure-Python Composable Workflow Authoring Layer

## Overview
Deliver `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md` — a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python authoring layer — and a one-line pointer edit in `docs/authoring.md`. The doc must let agents and humans tweak, refine, update, and combine ComfyUI workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy, and must reflect the user's settled decisions (typed handles, multi-stage `graph → Python → graph` orchestration, pinned-by-commit-SHA custom nodes, default session-reload for new node packs, manual/iterative ready-template migration, structured provenance, raw-JSON/raw-node escape hatches).

None of the four open flags indicate a wrong root cause or a wrong target file — they are spec-gap and wording fixes. Resolutions:
- **issue_hints** — name the hash types concretely: `git_commit_sha` (Git ref of the nodepack repo) and an optional `source_sha256` (SHA-256 of a node-class source file when a pack vendors classes from multiple upstream sources). Semantic labels resolve to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time.
- **correctness / FLAG-005** (same concern) — rename `external_python` to `ExternalPythonNode` and clarify it is **not** a variant of the authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`). At compile time it emits a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs/outputs. Include a one-line comparison table.
- **all_locations** — resolve the scope tension by **including** the `docs/authoring.md:99` one-line pointer edit in Step 2's scope. Two doc edits ship: the new plan plus a one-line pointer.

Current shape worth keeping in mind while writing the doc:
- `VibeWorkflow` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:74`) is the editable IR; compiles to API JSON via `compile("api")` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:170`) and optionally GraphBuilder via `compile("graphbuilder")` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:184`).
- `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38` defines a `Block` protocol returning `Handles`; concrete blocks under `vibecomfy/blocks/{loaders,sampling,latent,decode,save,encoding,video,subgraph}.py` use opaque positional widget keys. Critically, `@block` mutates the workflow at **authoring time**; it does not execute at Comfy runtime.
- `vibecomfy/patches/types.py:9` defines `Patch`. Splicing patches like `vibecomfy/patches/controlnet.py:45` use `disconnect`/`replace_edge`.
- Ready-template precedent: 41/42 embed `API_WORKFLOW` dicts (e.g. `ready_templates/image/z_image.py:6`), 1/42 is a native builder (`ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py:81`).
- Custom-node pinning today: `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md` already pin two packs by repo + Git commit SHA (`ComfyUI-KJNodes`, `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`).
- Prior decisions: GraphBuilder optional backend (`docs/graphbuilder_spike.md`); ComfyScript `learn` not `adopt` (`docs/comfyscript_spike.md`).
- Runtime is whole-graph only (`docs/runtime_surface.md`, `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`): HTTP `/prompt` or embedded `Comfy.queue_prompt_api(api_dict)`. There is no in-process Python "runs inside the graph" path; anything that needs to run during graph execution must compile to a Comfy node.

The deliverable is two markdown edits — one new doc and one one-line pointer. No code changes ship in this plan.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Re-confirm anchor evidence (`vibecomfy/workflow.py`, `vibecomfy/blocks/`, `ready_templates/`, `custom_nodes.lock`, `docs/authoring.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Re-read** the anchors so the doc cites accurate line numbers and current contracts: `vibecomfy/workflow.py:74` (IR), `vibecomfy/workflow.py:170` (api compile), `vibecomfy/workflow.py:184` (graphbuilder compile), `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12` (`Handles` wraps arbitrary values), `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38` (Block protocol — authoring-time only), `vibecomfy/blocks/_utils.py:8` (block-node helper + provenance metadata), `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47` (current string-ref handle convention `f"{node.id}.{slot}"`), `vibecomfy/patches/types.py:9` (Patch dataclass), and the ready-template precedent at `ready_templates/image/z_image.py:6` (API_WORKFLOW form) vs. `ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py:81` (native builder form).
2. **Verify** the migration scope counts to cite in the doc: `find ready_templates -name "*.py" -not -name "__init__.py" | wc -l` → 42; `grep -l "API_WORKFLOW" -r ready_templates --include="*.py" | wc -l` → 41; only `ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py` lacks `API_WORKFLOW`.
3. **Skim** `docs/authoring.md:99` (hand-curated-Python-builder policy) and `docs/custom_nodes.md` (existing pin format) so the reconciliation paragraph and pinning section reflect the exact existing wording.
4. **Locate** the precise insertion point in `docs/authoring.md` for the one-line pointer (immediately under the H1 or at the end of the "Ready Templates" section near `docs/authoring.md:99`).

### Step 2: Draft `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md` and add the pointer in `docs/authoring.md` (`docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`, `docs/authoring.md`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Write the file at exactly `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`** (not a kebab-cased title-derived name). The executor will only verify this exact path.
2. **Use this section skeleton** — every bullet from the brief and every settled decision from the user notes must appear as its own H2/H3 so reviewers can scan:
   - `# Python Composition DSL Plan`
   - `## Goals` — three to five bullets: pure-Python authoring; tweak / refine / update / combine; multi-stage orchestration (Comfy ⇄ ordinary Python); typed handles for debugging and provenance; works with HiddenSwitch+ComfyUI without forking either.
   - `## Non-Goals / Constraints` — not a new runtime; not a parallel IR (`VibeWorkflow` stays canonical); no live `/object_info` requirement at authoring time (snapshot OK); no single-node execution (HiddenSwitch only accepts whole-graph API dicts via `/prompt` and `Comfy.queue_prompt_api`); no arbitrary-Python injection inside an active Comfy graph.
   - `## Architecture` — one fenced-text diagram: `Python composition (new) → VibeWorkflow (IR) → compile("api") → HiddenSwitch (embedded or server)`, with `compile("graphbuilder")` as a parity branch and ComfyScript as an import-only adapter. Multi-stage flow: `Stage(Python) → Graph A → Stage(Python transform) → Graph B → Stage(Python finalize)`.
   - `## Layer Definitions` — (a) **Nodes** — typed `wf.node(class_type, **kwargs)` returning a typed `Handle` with `.out(name|index)` *and* a string-coerce path so existing `f"{node.id}.{slot}"` consumers keep working; (b) **Blocks** — existing `@block` functions, expanded; authoring-time only; (c) **Recipes** — higher-level pipelines that combine blocks; (d) **Patches** — decorate or splice; (e) **Templates** — published recipes (current `ready_templates/`); (f) **Stages** — Python-side steps in a multi-stage flow that consume typed media/file handles between graph executions; (g) **`ExternalPythonNode`** — a runtime-executable Comfy custom node compiled from Python (distinct from `@block`; see below); (h) **Escape hatches** — raw-JSON load (`convert_to_vibe_format`), raw-node `wf.add_node(...)`, raw API-dict pass-through.
   - `## Typed Handles (and Backward Compatibility)` — define a `Handle` object carrying `(node_id, output_slot, output_type | None, name | None)`. Make `str(handle)` return `f"{node_id}.{slot}"` so all current consumers in `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47`, `vibecomfy/blocks/_utils.py`, and recipe code keep working unchanged. New code can opt into `.out("image")` and `.type` accessors. Recipes adopt incrementally — no flag day. Cite that `Handles` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12`) currently wraps arbitrary values, so the typed `Handle` is additive.
   - `## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy ⇄ Python)` — primary mixing model is `Graph A → typed outputs/files/metadata → ordinary Python transform/decision → Graph B`. Rules: arbitrary Python runs *between* graph executions; arbitrary Python runs *inside* a graph only by compiling to an `ExternalPythonNode` (see next section). A `VibeFlow` object holds an ordered list of `Stage` entries (each is either a `VibeWorkflow` to execute or a `Callable[..., StageResult]`); the executor passes typed media handles between stages.
   - `## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from `@block`)` — explicit subsection clearing up the FLAG-005 / correctness concern. State that:
     1. The authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) **mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`**; it **never executes during Comfy runtime**.
     2. `ExternalPythonNode` is a **runtime** primitive: at compile time it emits a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs and outputs (typed file/tensor handles only — no Python objects across the boundary).
     3. The two are deliberately named differently to prevent collision. `@block` is authoring-only sugar; `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way to run Python inside an active graph.
     4. Include a one-line comparison table:

        | Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |
        | --- | --- | --- | --- |
        | `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |
        | `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/…) |
   - `## Custom Nodes — Adding On the Fly` — supported modes:
     1. **Pre-run install/discovery/sync** is in scope: extend `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` flows to install/sync pinned packs before a flow starts.
     2. **Hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor** is *not* the default — adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** (using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle: `stop()` → install → `start()`). Embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context; server sessions restart `comfyui serve`.
     3. **Optional Python import-and-load** path is allowed only for packs proven safe (no global registration side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init); behind a feature flag, never default.
   - `## Custom Nodes — Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` — pinning model with **named hash types** (resolves the issue_hints flag):
     - **`git_commit_sha`** (required, immutable): the Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo at the pinned version. This is the primary pin; `custom_nodes.lock` already records it for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper` (see `custom_nodes.md`).
     - **`semantic_label`** (optional, advisory): a tag, branch, or human label (e.g. `v1.4.0`, `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time. Never used at install time on its own.
     - **`source_sha256`** (optional, per-node-class): SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time. Used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources (e.g. nodepacks that re-export third-party code under a single repo); skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes.
     - State the distinction: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file. The two coexist; neither replaces the other.
     - Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields. A future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. The new doc references SD-006 from the user notes; it does not relitigate pinning strategy.
   - `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` — keep `compile("graphbuilder")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md`. Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target.
   - `## Relationship to ComfyScript` — `learn`, not `adopt`. Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script → `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (`docs/comfyscript_spike.md`).
   - `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` — HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout and on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.**
   - `## Custom Nodes — Authoring Surface` — schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`. Authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes — `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path. Hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step). Out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.
   - `## Ready Template Migration` — **state the precedent up front**: 42 ready-template `.py` files, 41 currently embed `API_WORKFLOW` (e.g. `ready_templates/image/z_image.py:6`), and **one is already a native VibeComfy Python builder — `ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py:81` — which we treat as the migration model**. Migration policy: (a) leave runtime-green API-dict templates untouched until they need editing; (b) when a template is being tweaked, re-author it through the composition layer using the Flux 4B builder shape; (c) keep round-trippability — every authored template must `compile("api")` to a graph that matches its prior runtime-green API JSON within an allowed-difference set (node IDs, MarkdownNote stripping). **Manual/iterative migration is the policy (user decision SD-008); no big-bang codemod.**
   - `## Reconciliation with docs/authoring.md` — short paragraph: `docs/authoring.md:99` says ready templates "should be hand-curated Python builders" and that retiring the materializer is intentional. The new plan **softens, not supersedes**: hand-curated Python builders remain the destination shape (Flux 4B is the proof point), but we accept the current 41-of-42 API-dict reality and migrate manually when a template is edited. **A one-line pointer added to `docs/authoring.md` in this same step links readers to this plan.** The core authoring rules in `docs/authoring.md` (blocks, patches, edge primitives, finalize_metadata) remain authoritative.
   - `## Provenance` — extend the existing `VibeNode.metadata` provenance (already records `block`, `block_id`, `widget_kwargs` per `vibecomfy/blocks/_utils.py:22`). Add: (i) `workflow.metadata["composition_trace"]` — ordered list of block/recipe/stage calls with kwargs; (ii) `workflow.source.provenance` (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:10`) populated with `{authored: true|false, recipe: "image.t2i", source_template: "image/z_image", builder_path: "<abs path>"}`; (iii) keep `metadata.subgraph_class_type` for opaque UUID subgraphs (`docs/authoring.md:84`); (iv) for multi-stage flows, write a `flow_trace` to `out/runs/<run-id>/metadata.json` recording each stage type, inputs, outputs, and node-pack lock SHAs (both `git_commit_sha` and any `source_sha256`).
   - `## Debugging` — three concrete affordances, gated by typed handles: (1) `wf.show()` / `vibecomfy analyze info` extended to print the composition trace; (2) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner — **available only after typed handles + schema-backed output types land**; (3) tap helpers (`wf.tap("save_image", from_handle)`) so authors can insert preview taps without rewriting downstream wiring. Reuse `analyze trace`/`analyze diff` (`docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:25`) for before/after diffs.
   - `## Escape Hatches` — three explicit escape paths, per user decision SD-007: (a) raw JSON load via `convert_to_vibe_format` (`vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py:19`); (b) raw node via `wf.add_node(class_type, **inputs)`; (c) raw API-dict pass-through (assemble a dict and call `Comfy.queue_prompt_api` directly). Each escape hatch loses some provenance; the doc states which fields go missing.
   - `## Rollout` — five phases, each gated on the previous:
     - **P1** — typed `Handle` API on top of current IR with string-coerce backward compat; `wf.node(...)` typed wrapper; parity tests against `compile("graphbuilder")`.
     - **P2** — expand block library coverage for Wan/LTX/KJNodes/GGUF/ACE Step; replace positional `widget_N` keys with named widgets where schema is known.
     - **P3** — `VibeFlow` multi-stage orchestration (Comfy ⇄ Python with typed media handles); `ExternalPythonNode` codegen for the rare cases that need Python at graph-execution time; custom-node session-reload helpers; `vibecomfy nodes lock` writing `git_commit_sha` + optional `semantic_label` + optional `source_sha256` to `custom_nodes.lock`.
     - **P4** — schema-backed validation against `/object_info` snapshot in CI (`docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:9`); typed-handle `output_type` populated; `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner ungated.
     - **P5** — manual/iterative ready-template migration starting from the highest-traffic templates, using the Flux 4B builder as the canonical shape; optional ComfyScript-import adapter and any further public docs.
   - `## Open Questions` — surface the questions in the planner output.
   - `## Assumptions` — surface the assumptions in the planner output.
3. **Add the one-line pointer in `docs/authoring.md`** at the location identified in Step 1.4. Suggested wording: `> See [docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md](python_composition_dsl_plan.md) for the broader composition-layer architecture this doc fits into.` This addresses the all_locations flag and prevents the two docs from drifting.
4. **Cite concrete file paths** with `path:line` references — at minimum: `vibecomfy/workflow.py:74`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py:170`, `vibecomfy/workflow.py:184`, `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12`, `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`, `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47`, `vibecomfy/blocks/_utils.py:22`, `vibecomfy/patches/types.py:9`, `ready_templates/image/z_image.py:6`, `ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py:81`, `vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py:19`, `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md`, `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`, `docs/authoring.md:99`, `docs/custom_nodes.md`, `custom_nodes.lock`. Reviewers should be able to click through.
5. **Keep length proportional** — target ~300–550 lines. This is a concise architecture plan, not a full RFC; do not restate the IR contract in detail (that lives in `docs/vibeworkflow.md`).

### Step 3: Verify the doc satisfies the brief and gate flags (`docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`, `docs/authoring.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Confirm** the file exists at exactly `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`.

   - `ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py:81` is cited as the existing native-builder precedent and the migration model, with the 42/41/1 count.
   - `wf.run_until(handle)` is explicitly gated on typed handles + schema-backed output types as a P4 prerequisite.
   - A backward-compat note states `Handle` objects coerce to existing `f"{node.id}.{slot}"` strings.
   - The reconciliation paragraph names `docs/authoring.md:99` and states the new plan **softens, not supersedes**.
   - **(issue_hints)** The Pinning section names `git_commit_sha`, optional `semantic_label`, and optional per-class `source_sha256`, and explains the distinction between Git repository-object hash and content digest.
   - **(correctness / FLAG-005)** The `ExternalPythonNode` subsection explicitly distinguishes it from authoring-time `@block` and includes the one-line comparison table.
   - **(all_locations)** A one-line pointer back to the new plan has been added to `docs/authoring.md`; verify with grep.
4. **Confirm** the doc takes a clear stance on each rollout phase and on the GraphBuilder/ComfyScript relationships consistent with `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` and `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`.

## Execution Order
1. Re-confirm anchor evidence and migration-scope counts so citations are accurate.
2. Draft the new doc and add the one-line pointer in `docs/authoring.md` in the same step (resolves the all_locations scope tension).
3. Verify path, headings, hash-type naming, `ExternalPythonNode` distinction, the pointer edit, and other flag-specific assertions.

## Validation Order
1. File-presence check at the exact contracted path.
2. Heading checklist via grep, including `ExternalPythonNode`.
3. Flag-resolution checklist (precedent cited, run_until gated, backward-compat noted, authoring.md reconciled, hash types named, ExternalPythonNode distinguished, pointer landed).
4. Spot-check that each cited `path:line` reference points at the asserted contract.

    {
      "id": "DEBT-018",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-106",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-107",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },

  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-013",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-108",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-145",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-110",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-142",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the supporting-infrastructure review still finds one missing check. in `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md`, sprint 2's verification matrix and risk register both single out `self_refiner` as a distinct drift-upgrade surface with its own smoke and its own silent-behavior-change risk, but the revised v4 body updates the file without keeping that supporting verification step in the actual execution checklist.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-109",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: the step 2 retry loop uses a naked `sleep 2` between attempts, which adds up to 6 seconds of latency on the happy path when the first patch eventually succeeds on attempt 2 or 3. for pods where supabase is reachable but slow during the initial moments of a cold runpod start, the retry logic is fine. however, if the first attempt fails with a tls handshake delay and the retry loop sleeps 2s per attempt regardless of whether curl itself has been blocking for tens of seconds, the total startup latency penalty could be substantial. this is a minor tuning concern, not a correctness issue.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-146",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: finding callers of the changed module shows that `shared.utils.self_refiner` is consumed by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx2_handler.py`, multiple ltx2 pipeline modules, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`. the revised validation steps still only drive the three bridge getters in `source/runtime/wgp_ports/vendor_imports.py`; none of the scheduled commands execute a real `self_refiner` caller, so the plan does not yet verify the main caller shapes for the other explicit sprint 2 file change.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
      ]
    }
  ],

  {
    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 6,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-007",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-008",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-018",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-106",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-107",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies \u2014 even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-143",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the path-mapping defect from the previous iteration is fixed, but a different technical gap remains around `self_refiner`. repository search shows `shared.utils.self_refiner` is imported by `wan2gp/wgp.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/distilled.py`, `ti2vid_one_stage.py`, `ti2vid_two_stages.py`, `wan2gp/models/ltx2/ltx_pipelines/utils/helpers.py`, and `wan2gp/models/wan/any2video.py`; the v4 plan still aligns `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` to upstream head without any concrete runtime step that exercises those code paths after the change, so the only repo-documented behavior check for that file is still missing from the executable body.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },

  {
    "subsystem": "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-013",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the package is internally inconsistent. the plan body says to keep `activepairdata` in `usestate` and make phase 2 optional, but the attached metadata and success criteria still describe the previous derive-via-`usememo` / remove-`setactivepairdata` / simplify-`onpairclick` plan. that means the approved-plan requirements are only partially coherent as presented.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-108",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the linux distro note is documentation-only; the generated install command in step 7 still runs `apt-get install python3.10-venv python3.10-dev ffmpeg` without any pre-check that the package exists. users on ubuntu 24.04+ who ignore the doc and try to copy-paste the command will hit a confusing `e: unable to locate package python3.10-venv` from apt rather than a targeted error from commandutils.ts. a one-line detection pre-check (e.g., `apt-cache show python3.10-venv >/dev/null 2>&1 || { echo 'install deadsnakes ppa first \u2014 see readme'; exit 1; }`) would convert the silent failure into an actionable error, but the plan does not add this.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-145",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: checked the revised v4 body against `docs/wan2gp_fork_migration_plan.md` sprint 2. the repo plan doc still lists an \"ltx-2 `self_refiner` smoke against the pre-sprint behavioral baseline\" as a sprint 2 verification gate and repeats that smoke in the functionality-preservation checks, but the revised execution steps no longer schedule that smoke anywhere; it survives only as an info-level metadata criterion. because `wan2gp/shared/utils/self_refiner.py` remains an explicit sprint 2 deliverable, the revised plan still only partially carries forward the verification package described in the source migration plan.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "execute-sprint-2-of-the-20260421-2202"
        ]
      }
    ]
  },
  {
    "subsystem": "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure",
    "total_occurrences": 3,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-014",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 1 still misses a real supporting-data location: `trailingpairdata` lives outside `pairdatabyindex` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotpresentationadapter.ts#l111), but the proposed sync logic only considers `pairdatabyindex`. if trailing slot regenerate is part of the bug surface, that location is not covered.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "fix-the-denormalized-20260331-0225"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-110",
        "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: the non-swallowing helper `update_worker_phase_strict` is a new shell function, but phase 1 step 2 introduces it only as pseudo-code. there is no concrete note that the helper must handle supabase http non-2xx responses as failures (curl returns 0 even on http 4xx/5xx by default unless `-f` or `--fail-with-body` is used). if the implementer ports `update_worker_phase` verbatim with only `|| return 0` removed, a 500 from supabase would still report success because curl's exit code is 0. the plan should specify `curl --fail` or explicit http status code checking in the strict variant.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "migrate-the-python-worker-20260409-0353"
        ]
      },

        "id": "DEBT-142",
